# 10db M3 Knowledge Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Every managed session starts, continues after a handoff and recovers from an emergency compaction with a small, deterministic context pack of the confirmed principles and recorded decisions that apply to its task; agents propose principles and the builder confirms them with a scope; and the builder lists, searches, filters, records, supersedes, retires and withdraws decisions and principles, and reads where each one came from, in a Knowledge view.

**Architecture:**

- **Data.** A new `principles` table (statement, kind, topic key, scope, status, supersession, source quote and speaker, confirmation, retirement and a withdrawn tombstone) with its own FTS5 index, made by a second hand-written migration in the regenerated baseline. `decisions` gain a withdrawn tombstone; `proposals` and `deviations` gain a link to a principle. A record's state (`proposed`, `current`, `superseded`, `retired`, `withdrawn`) is derived on read, never stored (spec §4 invariants).
- **Search.** All full-text queries move into one module, `packages/db/src/store/knowledge-search.ts` (`packages/db/AGENTS.md`: "Full-text search lives in one module"). It searches current records by default and the whole history on request, and a startup health check verifies both FTS indexes against their tables and rebuilds a broken one.
- **Knowledge module.** A new daemon module, `apps/daemon/src/knowledge/`, owns the rules: scope (which records apply to a task), the packer (a pure, deterministic function that fills a token budget), principle proposals and their verdicts, recording a decision from an answer, the always/never detector, the override review and provenance. It imports `@10db/contracts` and `@10db/db` only.
- **Context pack.** `briefing/context-pack.ts` reads the candidate records and hands them to the packer. The pack carries policies, then heuristics, then decisions (FTS plus area match), with per-shelf dropped counts. The bootstrap prompt renders it as rules the agent can cite; the post-compaction re-injection renders the same text. Each injection appends a `context.packed` event with what was used and dropped.
- **Capture.** `propose` with kind `principle` carries a structured draft and raises a card whose options pick the scope. An answer can record a decision at any scope or skip it. An answer's "always"/"never" sentences become principle proposals. Two signed overrides of one principle in 30 days raise a "review this principle?" card.
- **API and UI.** Knowledge routes for decisions, principles, proposals and provenance. A top-level Knowledge view (spec §10) with Principles, Decisions and Proposals tabs, search, scope filters, a history switch, record, supersede, retire and withdraw forms, and a provenance drawer. The inbox card shows a principle proposal's scope choices and a "Record as a decision" control on question cards.

**Tech Stack:** As in M2a and M2b Part A. No new dependencies.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-13-10db-factory-loop-design.md`. This plan implements:

- Decision 17: decisions and principles with you, project or area scope; agents only propose principles; supersession by a fixed key; provenance on every write.
- §4 Knowledge: the `principles` table and the knowledge invariants ("Knowledge status is derived on read … Supersession uses fixed keys").
- §6: `get_context`, `find_knowledge`, `record_decision` and `propose`, extended additively; `report_deviation` gains an optional principle key.
- §9: the context pack (order, budget, `budget_used` and `dropped_count`, explicit emptiness and truncation, injection at start and after compaction), capture (inbox answers become decisions; always/never statements become principle proposals with a choice of scope; two signed overrides raise a review card), provenance and withdrawn tombstones.
- §10 Knowledge: decisions, principles and pending proposals, filtered by scope.
- §12: "every write is read back via search in tests, plus a runtime health check".
- §13 item 4: "Context pack, decisions and principles UI, capture flows".
- Open item "Spec §9: the context pack uses FTS only; area match is not built" is closed by Task 6.

**Builds on (execution order).** This plan executes after **both** of these are on `main`, on a branch cut from that `main`:

1. **M2b Part A** (`feat/m2b-board-and-settings`, plan `07-m2b-board-and-settings`): the board at `/board`, `useSelectedProject` in `apps/desktop/src/shell/selected-project.ts`, the Settings shell over a section registry (`apps/desktop/src/views/settings/sections.ts`), the `board_columns` table and decisions 70–76.
2. **Batch delivery** (plan `08-batch-delivery`, on `origin/docs/batch-delivery-design`; code on `feat/batch-delivery`): the `batches` table, delivery fields on projects, `ProjectViewDto` from `GET /projects`, `onFlagAnswer` in `InboxService`, `BatchUpdated` events, and decisions 77–85. Its schema change regenerates the migration baseline (decision 67); this plan regenerates it again on top.

Where a file, signature or anchor named here doesn't match the code on `main` after those merges, trust the code, adapt the step and say so in the task's report. This plan was written against `origin/main` at `2b3d811`, `feat/m2b-board-and-settings` at `53095a9` and `origin/docs/batch-delivery-design` at `08b2d19`.

**Plan series:** `00-spikes` … `06-m2a-teams-and-routing`, `07-m2b-board-and-settings`, `08-batch-delivery`, then the plans numbered 09–10 written in parallel, and **`11-m3-knowledge` (this file)**.

**Not in this plan:**

- **The legacy harness import** (spec §4 "Legacy harness import", §13 item 4). It reads the builder's private repos, so it gets its own later plan, `m3-legacy-import`: ROADMAP blocks to tasks, `plans/<slug>/` files to decisions, deviations and handoffs with `sources` rows, ledgers and `AGENTS.md` sections to proposed principles and decisions, the originals copied into the data folder, the bulk confirm in the Knowledge view (spec §10 "Bulk confirm for imports"), and the uncommitted acceptance script (spec §12). This plan leaves it two seams: `insertPrinciple` with status `proposed`, and `SourceKind` gains its `import` value there.
- **§1.3 on the pilot project.** It is a manual live run after the import plan; `docs/open-items.md` keeps its row.
- **Facts** (spec §1.4: facts UI and injection are the fast-follow; §13 item 6 "Next … the facts UI"). The pre-release baseline is regenerated anyway, so the facts plan adds the table, its writes, its UI and its pack shelf together. See Open decision 5.
- **PDF intake and embeddings** (spec §1.4).
- **"Answered from D-n"** (spec §7: before a question reaches the builder, existing records that answer it are returned instead). See Open decision 6.
- **The `SessionStart` hook for terminal sessions** (spec §9). Terminal sessions are the takeover plan's; it injects the same `buildContextPack` output, rendered as `compactionReinjection` renders it (Task 6).

## New decisions

Task 15 appends these to `docs/architecture/decisions.md` as consecutive rows. Main ends at 69, M2b Part A takes 70–76 and batch delivery 77–85; K1–K12 take the next free numbers at merge (86 onward unless another plan lands first). The code follows them from Task 1; comments cite the spec, never these labels.

| Label | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Why                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ----- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| K1    | A decision or principle has a target: `you` (no project; it applies in every project), `project`, or `area` (a project and one of its area names). Its state is derived on read: `withdrawn` if it has a tombstone; for a principle, `proposed` while unconfirmed; `superseded` if a successor that is not withdrawn (and, for a principle, not proposed) names it in `supersedes_id`; `retired` for a retired principle; otherwise `current`. Nothing is hard-deleted: withdrawing needs a reason and keeps the row. Only a current record (or a proposed principle, which rejecting its proposal withdraws) can be withdrawn; anything else is a 409, so withdrawing a superseded link never makes its predecessor current beside its successor.                                                                                                                                                       | Spec §4 invariants and §9 provenance. One rule for every shelf keeps the pack, search and the UI agreeing.                                                                          |
| K2    | A principle is a statement, a kind (`policy` = must, `heuristic` = prefer, `belief` = context) and a topic key: lowercase words joined by `.` or `-`, at most 80 characters. A key made from a statement with no Latin letters or digits is `untitled-` plus six hex digits of a hash of the statement, so two such statements never share a key. Confirming a principle supersedes the current confirmed principle with the same topic key **and the same target**; principles with different targets never supersede each other. Agents only propose; the builder confirms (from the card or the Knowledge view) or creates a confirmed principle directly. Rejecting a proposal withdraws its proposed principle with the rejection note as the reason.                                                                                                                                               | Spec decision 17 and §4 "Supersession uses fixed keys … never similarity". A key shared across targets would let a project rule silently replace one of the builder's own.          |
| K3    | A decision is superseded only by an explicit link: the builder's Supersede action records a new decision whose `supersedes_id` names it, at any target. Only a current decision can be superseded or withdrawn (409 otherwise), so the chain stays linear: withdrawing its head makes the link before it current again, and no other link can be withdrawn.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Decisions have no natural fixed key; the builder's explicit choice is the fixed key. Re-scoping a decision is a supersede.                                                          |
| K4    | The context pack's knowledge holds, in order: confirmed current policies in scope, then heuristics, then the current decisions relevant to the task, filled greedily into `CONTEXT_PACK_BUDGET_TOKENS` = 1500 estimated tokens (characters ÷ 4). In scope means `you`, `project`, or `area` for an area the task lists or the workstream holds a live claim on. Beliefs are not packed and are set aside first, so a belief never hides a rule; then, for one topic key, only the most specific policies and heuristics enter (area, then project, then `you`). Relevant decisions are, in order: area-matched FTS hits, other FTS hits by rank, area-matched decisions with no hit, newest first; when there are none, the newest current decisions in scope. A record that doesn't fit is skipped and the next one tried; the pack counts what each shelf dropped and states truncation and emptiness. | Spec §9 "Context pack". The budget stays small (gbrain's always-loaded core memory failed its eval); 1000 tokens fitted decisions alone, and principles are shorter than decisions. |
| K5    | Each injection appends a `context.packed` event (entity `session`) with the reason, the budget, the tokens used and the dropped counts. Every session's bootstrap prompt carries the pack, so every session start is an injection: reason `start` for a new conversation, `resume` for one that continues an earlier conversation (a resume or a delivered answer), and `compaction` after an emergency compaction.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Spec §9 "It records `budget_used` and `dropped_count`". The event log is the record; no new column.                                                                                 |
| K6    | `find_knowledge` searches current decisions and current confirmed principles of the project (all its areas, plus `you`), labels each hit with its scope and area, and takes an optional `kind` (`any`, `decision`, `principle`). Its `limit` applies to each shelf: up to `limit` decisions and up to `limit` principles. History never reaches an agent.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Spec §6. An agent searching on purpose may need a neighbouring area's record; the label lets it judge. The pack, which is not asked for, stays strict.                              |
| K7    | `propose` with kind `principle` takes an optional draft (statement, kind, topic key, scope, area, quote). 10db stores a `proposed` principle, a pending proposal linked to it and a proposal card whose options are `accept_project`, `accept_you`, `accept_area` (only when the draft names an area) and `reject`. A draft without a topic key gets one made from its statement. The Knowledge view's Accept can change the statement, kind, topic key and target first; an area target must be one of the project's areas. Both paths run one function and close the card; a second verdict on a resolved proposal is a 409. A card closed from the Knowledge view records the verdict it offered for that target, or `accept` with the target in its note when it never offered one.                                                                                                                  | Spec §9 "with a choice of scope" and §7 "Proposal" cards. Keeping the option labels stable values (like permission choices) lets the UI show words.                                 |
| K8    | An inbox answer records a decision as before (a question in a product, design or engineering category, at project scope), unless the answer says otherwise: `record: null` records nothing; `record: { scope, area }` records it at that target for a question card in any category.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Spec §9 "Inbox answers become decisions"; the builder knows when an answer is a one-off.                                                                                            |
| K9    | Until the PM agent exists, a deterministic detector turns each sentence of an answer's note that contains the word "always" or "never" into a principle proposal (kind `policy`, topic key made from the sentence, scope `project`, the sentence as the quote, speaker `me`), at most three per answer. The PM agent will later propose through the same `propose` path.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Spec §9 "The PM proposes principles from always/never statements in answers". A word rule is predictable and nothing is applied without the builder's confirmation.                 |
| K10   | `report_deviation` takes an optional `principleKey`, resolved like the pack resolves it: the most specific current policy or heuristic with that key in the workstream's scope (a belief is never packed, so it can't be departed from; an unknown key is an error). When a principle has two or more deviations naming it within 30 days, 10db raises one folded flag card "Review this principle?" with `keep` and `retire`; `retire` retires it with the answer's note as the reason.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Spec §9 "Two recent signed overrides of a principle raise a 'review this principle?' card".                                                                                         |
| K11   | Amends 67. At start, after migrations, the daemon runs FTS5's `integrity-check` (content included) on `decisions_fts` and `principles_fts`; an index that disagrees with its table is logged and rebuilt. A check or rebuild that fails is logged and the daemon keeps running; once the inbox exists, each project gets one folded "Knowledge search is broken" flag card (the inbox has no card without a project), and the next start tries again. While the decision index is broken, the context pack skips the task search and carries the newest decisions, so briefings keep working. The baseline gains a second hand-written migration: it is now `0000_init` (generated) plus `0001_decisions_fts` and `0002_principles_fts`, each authored once and kept byte for byte.                                                                                                                      | Spec §12 "plus a runtime health check" and "a parse failure becomes a flagged event, never a daemon crash"; decision 67.                                                            |
| K12   | Knowledge is a top-level view at `/knowledge` (spec §10 lists it beside Board, Factory, Inbox and Settings) for the selected project, with Principles, Decisions and Proposals tabs, a search box, a scope filter (all in scope, you, project, one area), a history switch and a provenance drawer.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Spec §10. The project switcher is the one the board and Settings share.                                                                                                             |

## Open decisions for Travis

The spec leaves these open. The plan is written against each recommended default; changing one changes the tasks named.

1. **Who proposes principles from "always/never" answers before the PM agent exists.** Default: the deterministic detector of K9 (Task 8). Alternative: nothing until the PM agent; the builder adds principles by hand.
2. **Beliefs in the pack.** Default: not packed; `find_knowledge` returns them (K4, K6; Task 5). Alternative: a third principle shelf after heuristics.
3. **The pack budget.** Default: one constant, 1500 estimated tokens, no per-project setting (Task 5). Alternative: a `contextBudgetTokens` project field and a Settings control (adds to `ProjectDto`, a shared hot spot).
4. **One topic key at several scopes.** Default: the most specific wins in the pack (K4); all of them stay listed in the Knowledge view. Alternative: pack all of them.
5. **The facts table.** Spec §1.4 says the facts schema is "in this slice" but §13 names no milestone for it. Default: out of M3; the facts plan adds the table with its writes, UI and pack shelf. Alternative: add the empty `facts` table now (Task 2 grows by one table).
6. **"Answered from D-n"** (spec §7). Default: out of this plan; deciding that a record answers a question needs a rule that can't answer wrongly, and FTS similarity is the kind of match decision 17 forbids for supersession. A later plan can match on a fixed key (a question's category plus a normalized title). Alternative: in M3, with FTS top hit plus builder confirmation.
7. **The override review threshold.** Default: two deviations naming the principle within 30 days (K10, Task 9). Alternative: any two, ever.
8. **Rejecting a principle proposal.** Default: the proposed principle is withdrawn with the rejection note (K2), so the status vocabulary stays the spec's three. Alternative: a fourth stored status, `rejected`.
9. **`find_knowledge` scope.** Default: the whole project plus `you`, labelled (K6). Alternative: the same strict scope as the pack.
10. **Withdrawing a superseded record.** Default: refused with a 409; only the head of a chain (or a proposed principle) can be withdrawn, so a chain never forks into two current records (K1, K3; Tasks 3–4). To undo a middle link, withdraw the newer ones first. Alternative: allow it and re-link the successor to the withdrawn record's predecessor.
11. **`context.packed` for resumed sessions.** Every session's bootstrap prompt carries the pack, a resumed conversation's included, so the default records every session start: reason `start` for a new conversation, `resume` for one that continues (K5, Task 6). The plan review suggested recording only for sessions that weren't briefed from a handoff, but a session continuing from a handoff is exactly one whose prompt carries the pack. Alternative: record only `start` and `compaction`.

## Global Constraints

Everything in M0's, M1a's, M1b's, M2a's, M2b Part A's and batch delivery's Global Constraints still applies. On top of that:

- **Branch:** `feat/m3-knowledge`, cut from `main` after M2b Part A and batch delivery have both merged. Open one PR to `main` at the end; its title is a Conventional Commit (`feat(daemon): …` or similar).
- **Commits:**
  - Each commit is one command that names its paths: `git add <path> … && git commit -m '…'`. Never `git add -A`, `git add .` or `git commit -a`.
  - The scope is one of `daemon`, `desktop`, `contracts`, `db`, `harness`, `repo`, `ci`, `docs`, `deps`. The subject is lowercase and the header is at most 100 characters. Single quotes for `-m`.
  - Every message ends with the attribution footer your own session's instructions give (for Claude Code, a `Co-Authored-By:` line and a session link), after a blank line. Add it to each commit command below as extra `-m` paragraphs; the commands show only the subject, because the footer belongs to the session that runs them, not the one that wrote this plan.
  - Run `pnpm format` first. A failing hook leaves the paths staged: fix the cause and rerun the same line. Never `--no-verify` or `LEFTHOOK=0`.
- **Generated files** are regenerated, never edited: `pnpm --filter @10db/contracts generate` after a contracts change. A schema change regenerates the migration baseline (`packages/db/AGENTS.md`, decision 67), with the new `0002_principles_fts.sql` written once in Task 2 and then kept byte for byte. After Task 2, delete any local `.dev/data`: the daemon refuses a database made under the old baseline and exits with code 3 (the E2E run recreates its own data directory).
- **Contracts are additive** (`packages/contracts/AGENTS.md`): new schemas, new optional input fields (`InboxAnswer.record`, `find_knowledge.kind`, `propose.principle`, `report_deviation.principleKey`), new output fields (`KnowledgeHit.scope` and `area`, `PrincipleHit`, the pack's new shelves, `find_knowledge.principles`, `propose.principleId` with a `null` default so a recorded pre-upgrade result still parses), three event types and one ID kind. `PROTOCOL_VERSION` stays 2.
- **IDs** are minted only through `mintId`. New kind: `Principle` (`pri`).
- **Every state change writes its row and its event in one transaction** (`state.write`). New entity types `principle` and `decision` (already emitted by `decision.recorded`) and `proposal` are mapped in the desktop's `INVALIDATIONS` (Task 11).
- **Every knowledge write is read back through search in its test** (`.claude/rules/testing.md`): a test that inserts, confirms, supersedes, retires or withdraws a decision or principle asserts what `searchDecisions`, `searchPrinciples`, `find_knowledge` or the list routes' `q` return afterwards, not only the row.
- **The packer is pure.** `apps/daemon/src/knowledge/pack.ts`, `scope.ts` and `standing-rules.ts` import only `@10db/contracts`: no `@10db/db`, no `node:*`, no clock. Their callers pass rows in.
- **Untrusted text stays data** (spec §7): statements, quotes, questions and rationales from agents are rendered as text in the UI and as quoted bullet lines in prompts, never as instructions to 10db.
- **No `as` casts, no `!`, no `any`** except at a validated boundary with a comment (`.claude/rules/code-style.md`). Exhaustive switches over `KnowledgeScope`, `KnowledgeState`, `PrincipleKind`, `PrincipleVerdict` and `PrincipleReviewChoice`.
- **Tests** (`.claude/rules/testing.md`): red first with the expected failure written in each step; table-driven for scope, packing, topic keys, verdicts, the detector and the review threshold; a real SQLite file in a temp directory; the fake adapter is the only Claude boundary; desktop tests hit the daemon URL through MSW; no sleeps.
- **Tunable values are named constants:** `CONTEXT_PACK_BUDGET_TOKENS = 1500`, `CHARS_PER_TOKEN = 4`, `PACK_SEARCH_LIMIT = 20`, `PACK_RECENT_LIMIT = 5`, `MAX_STANDING_RULES = 3`, `OVERRIDE_REVIEW_COUNT = 2`, `OVERRIDE_REVIEW_WINDOW_DAYS = 30`, `TOPIC_KEY_MAX = 80`.
- **knip:** an `index.ts` re-exports only what another module or a test imports.
- **Comments never carry decision numbers or labels** (`.claude/rules/code-style.md`); they cite the spec (`spec §9`) or say the why in words.

## Review Focus

These are the conditions most likely to bite a person, each with the task whose tests pin it:

1. **A superseded, retired, withdrawn or still-proposed record reaches an agent.**
   - Case: the builder supersedes "Use BullMQ" with "Use pg-boss", retires a policy, withdraws a decision, or an agent's proposal sits unconfirmed; or the builder withdraws the middle link of a three-link chain, which would make its predecessor current beside its successor.
   - Neither the pack nor `find_knowledge` returns any of them; the Knowledge view shows them only with the history switch on, labelled with their state. Only the head of a chain can be withdrawn.
   - Tests: Task 3 (`supersedes a current decision: search returns only the successor, history both`; `withdrawing a superseded record is refused`), Task 4 (`never returns a proposed, retired, superseded or withdrawn principle from a current search`; `withdrawing a superseded record is refused`), Task 6 (`packs no superseded, withdrawn, retired or proposed record`), Task 7 (`proposes a principle as a proposed record with a scope-choice card, unseen by find_knowledge until confirmed`).
2. **A record from another project or another area leaks into a pack.**
   - Case: project B's policy, or project A's `ui`-area rule, while a task in project A claims only `api`.
   - Not packed. A `you` principle is packed everywhere; an area principle only for a task that lists that area or holds its claim.
   - Tests: Task 5 (scope table), Task 6 (`packs only records that apply to this project and the task’s areas`).
3. **A knowledge write that search can't find.**
   - Case: hostile query text (`what's -the "deal" (NEAR OR`), an FTS index out of step with its table after a crash or a manual edit, a principle confirmed with edits.
   - Search quotes every term; every write in this plan is read back through search; a broken index is detected at start and rebuilt, and one that can't be rebuilt is logged and flagged in the inbox while the daemon keeps running and the pack falls back to the newest decisions.
   - Tests: Task 3 (`detects an index out of step with its table and rebuilds it`; the hostile-query rows already in `inbox-knowledge.test.ts` keep running against the moved search), Task 4 (`finds a principle by its edited statement after confirmation`), Task 10 (`logs a rebuild that fails on a corrupt shadow table`, `keeps running when a search index cannot be rebuilt at start`), Task 6 (`still briefs from policies and the newest decisions when the decision search index is broken`), and every write test.
4. **The budget hides a policy without saying so.**
   - Case: twenty long policies, or one rule longer than the whole budget.
   - Policies are packed first; whatever doesn't fit is counted per shelf, and the prompt says how many of each were left out and to call `find_knowledge`; an empty pack says so.
   - Tests: Task 5 (budget table), Task 6 (`fills the budget and counts what each shelf dropped`, `says what each shelf dropped, and never claims nothing applies when the budget hid it`).
5. **The same proposal is decided twice.**
   - Case: the builder accepts in the Knowledge view while the card is open in the Inbox, or double-clicks Accept.
   - The first verdict wins; the second answers 409 and changes nothing; the card is resolved once.
   - Tests: Task 8 (`refuses a second verdict on a decided proposal`), Task 10 (`answers 409 to a second accept`).
6. **Two principles with one topic key at different targets.**
   - Case: the builder's own `testing.no-sleeps` (`you`) and a project's `testing.no-sleeps`; or an `api`-area belief that shares a project policy's key.
   - Neither supersedes the other; the pack carries only the project one for that project's tasks; both stay current in the Knowledge view. A belief is set aside before the most specific record is chosen, so it never hides a policy or heuristic.
   - Tests: Task 4 (`supersedes only within the same topic key and target`), Task 5 (`keeps only the most specific principle per topic key`, `never lets a belief hide a rule`), Task 9 (`resolves a key to the most specific current principle for the areas`).

---

## Shared hot spots

M2b Part A, batch delivery and the plans written in parallel touch the same files. Every change here is additive. Whichever branch merges second rebases on the other and **re-runs the generators** (the baseline regeneration in Task 2 Step 4, then `pnpm --filter @10db/contracts generate`); it never resolves a conflict in `packages/db/drizzle/` or `packages/contracts/generated/` by hand.

### Task 1: Knowledge contracts

New vocabularies and schemas, standalone: no existing schema changes shape except an optional field on `InboxAnswer`, so nothing downstream breaks. The MCP and context-pack additions land in the tasks that produce them (Tasks 6, 7 and 9).

**Files:**

- Modify: `packages/contracts/src/states.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/ids.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/events.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/entities.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/index.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/json-schema.ts`
- Create: `packages/contracts/src/knowledge.ts`
- Test: `packages/contracts/src/knowledge.test.ts`
- Generated: `packages/contracts/generated/*.schema.json`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `idSchema`, `ID_PREFIX` in `ids.ts`; `Name` in `entities.ts`; `ProposalKind`, `SourceKind` in `states.ts`.
- Produces (used by every later task):
  - Vocabularies in `states.ts`: `KnowledgeScope` (`you | project | area`), `PrincipleKind` (`policy | heuristic | belief`), `PrincipleStatus` (`proposed | confirmed | retired`), `KnowledgeState` (`proposed | current | superseded | retired | withdrawn`), `ProposalStatus` (`pending | accepted | rejected`), `PrincipleVerdict` (`accept_project | accept_you | accept_area | reject`), `PrincipleReviewChoice` (`keep | retire`).
  - `PrincipleId` (`pri_<uuidv7>`), `mintId('Principle')`.
  - `EventType.DecisionChanged` (`decision.changed`), `EventType.PrincipleSaved` (`principle.saved`), `EventType.ContextPacked` (`context.packed`).
  - `entities.ts`: `AreaName`, `DecisionRecording` (`{ scope: KnowledgeScope; area: string | null }`), `InboxAnswer.record?: DecisionRecording | null`.
  - `knowledge.ts`: `TOPIC_KEY_MAX`, `TopicKey`, `topicKeyFrom(text: string): TopicKey`, `KnowledgeTarget`, `targetColumns(t: KnowledgeTarget): TargetColumns`, `TargetColumns` (`{ scope; projectId: ProjectId | null; area: string | null }`), `DecisionInput`, `DecisionState`, `DecisionDto`, `PrincipleStatement`, `PrincipleDraft`, `PrincipleInput`, `PrincipleDto`, `ReasonInput`, `ProposalDto`, `ProposalAccept`, `ProposalReject`, `ProvenanceDto`, `KnowledgeQuery`, `ProposalQuery`, `PRINCIPLE_REVIEW_DETAIL = 'principleReview'`.

```ts
/** Who a knowledge record applies to (spec §9): the builder everywhere, one project, or one area of it. */
export const KnowledgeScope = { You: 'you', Project: 'project', Area: 'area' } as const;
export type KnowledgeScope = ValueOf<typeof KnowledgeScope>;

/** policy = must, heuristic = prefer, belief = context (spec §4). */
export const PrincipleKind = {
  Policy: 'policy',
  Heuristic: 'heuristic',
  Belief: 'belief',
} as const;
export type PrincipleKind = ValueOf<typeof PrincipleKind>;

/** What is stored; whether a confirmed principle is still current is derived on read. */
export const PrincipleStatus = {
  Proposed: 'proposed',
  Confirmed: 'confirmed',
  Retired: 'retired',
} as const;
export type PrincipleStatus = ValueOf<typeof PrincipleStatus>;

/** A record's state, derived on read and never stored (spec §4 invariants). */
export const KnowledgeState = {
  Proposed: 'proposed',
  Current: 'current',
  Superseded: 'superseded',
  Retired: 'retired',
  Withdrawn: 'withdrawn',
} as const;
export type KnowledgeState = ValueOf<typeof KnowledgeState>;

export const ProposalStatus = {
  Pending: 'pending',
  Accepted: 'accepted',
  Rejected: 'rejected',
} as const;
export type ProposalStatus = ValueOf<typeof ProposalStatus>;

/** A principle proposal card's options: accept at a scope, or reject. Stable values; the UI shows words. */
export const PrincipleVerdict = {
  AcceptProject: 'accept_project',
  AcceptYou: 'accept_you',
  AcceptArea: 'accept_area',
  Reject: 'reject',
} as const;
export type PrincipleVerdict = ValueOf<typeof PrincipleVerdict>;

/** A "review this principle?" card's options (spec §9). */
export const PrincipleReviewChoice = { Keep: 'keep', Retire: 'retire' } as const;
export type PrincipleReviewChoice = ValueOf<typeof PrincipleReviewChoice>;
```

/** Recording an answer as a decision at a target; an area scope names its area, any other scope none. */
export const DecisionRecording = z
  .object({ scope: z.enum(KnowledgeScope), area: AreaName.nullable() })
  .refine((r) => (r.scope === KnowledgeScope.Area) === (r.area !== null), {
    message: 'area is required for scope "area" and only allowed there',
    path: ['area'],
  });
export type DecisionRecording = z.infer<typeof DecisionRecording>;

export const InboxAnswer = z.object({
  choice: z.string().min(1),
  note: z.string().max(4000).optional(),
  /**
   * Omitted: a product, design or engineering question is recorded as a project decision (spec §7).
   * Null: not recorded. A recording: recorded at that target, whatever the question's category.
   */
  record: DecisionRecording.nullable().optional(),
});

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the knowledge schemas**

`packages/contracts/src/knowledge.ts`:

```ts
import { z } from 'zod';
import { AreaName } from './entities';
import {
  DecisionId,
  InboxItemId,
  PrincipleId,
  ProjectId,
  ProposalId,
  SessionId,
  SourceId,
  WorkstreamId,
} from './ids';
import {
  KnowledgeScope,
  KnowledgeState,
  PrincipleKind,
  PrincipleStatus,
  ProposalKind,
  ProposalStatus,
  SourceKind,
} from './states';

const Timestamp = z.iso.datetime();

export const TOPIC_KEY_MAX = 80;

/** The fixed key a principle supersedes by (spec §4): lowercase words joined by `.` or `-`. */
export const TopicKey = z
  .string()
  .max(TOPIC_KEY_MAX)
  .regex(/^[a-z0-9]+(?:[.-][a-z0-9]+)*$/, 'lowercase words joined by . or -');
export type TopicKey = z.infer<typeof TopicKey>;

/** FNV-1a over the text's UTF-16 code units: a short stable tag, not a security hash. */
function shortHash(text: string): string {
  let hash = 0x811c9dc5;
  for (let i = 0; i < text.length; i += 1) {
    hash ^= text.charCodeAt(i);
    hash = Math.imul(hash, 0x01000193) >>> 0;
  }
  return hash.toString(16).padStart(8, '0').slice(0, 6);
}

/**
 * A topic key made from free text, whole words up to the limit; the builder can change it before
 * confirming. Text with no Latin letters or digits gets `untitled-` and a hash of the text, so two such
 * statements never share a key and the second never silently supersedes the first.
 */
export function topicKeyFrom(text: string): TopicKey {
  const words = text.toLowerCase().match(/[a-z0-9]+/g) ?? [];
  let key = '';
  for (const word of words) {
    const next = key === '' ? word : `${key}-${word}`;
    if (next.length > TOPIC_KEY_MAX) {
      if (key === '') key = word.slice(0, TOPIC_KEY_MAX);
      break;
    }
    key = next;
  }
  return TopicKey.parse(key === '' ? `untitled-${shortHash(text.trim())}` : key);
}

/** Who a record applies to: the builder in every project, one project, or one area of a project. */
export const KnowledgeTarget = z.discriminatedUnion('scope', [
  z.object({ scope: z.literal(KnowledgeScope.You) }),
  z.object({ scope: z.literal(KnowledgeScope.Project), projectId: ProjectId }),
  z.object({ scope: z.literal(KnowledgeScope.Area), projectId: ProjectId, area: AreaName }),
]);
export type KnowledgeTarget = z.infer<typeof KnowledgeTarget>;

export interface TargetColumns {
  readonly scope: KnowledgeScope;
  readonly projectId: ProjectId | null;
  readonly area: string | null;
}

/** The stored columns for a target. A `you` record has no project, so it applies in every project. */
export function targetColumns(target: KnowledgeTarget): TargetColumns {
  switch (target.scope) {
    case KnowledgeScope.You:
      return { scope: target.scope, projectId: null, area: null };
    case KnowledgeScope.Project:
      return { scope: target.scope, projectId: target.projectId, area: null };
    case KnowledgeScope.Area:
      return { scope: target.scope, projectId: target.projectId, area: target.area };
  }
}

export const DecisionInput = z.object({
  target: KnowledgeTarget,
  question: z.string().trim().min(1).max(2000),
  choice: z.string().trim().min(1).max(2000),
  rationale: z.string().trim().min(1).max(4000),
  /** The answered card this decision comes from; its provenance then points at the card. */
  fromInboxId: InboxItemId.optional(),
});
export type DecisionInput = z.infer<typeof DecisionInput>;

export const DecisionState = z.enum([
  KnowledgeState.Current,
  KnowledgeState.Superseded,
  KnowledgeState.Withdrawn,
]);
export type DecisionState = z.infer<typeof DecisionState>;

export const DecisionDto = z.object({
  id: DecisionId,
  projectId: ProjectId.nullable(),
  scope: z.enum(KnowledgeScope),
  area: z.string().nullable(),
  question: z.string(),
  choice: z.string(),
  rationale: z.string(),
  decidedBy: z.string(),
  supersedesId: DecisionId.nullable(),
  supersededById: DecisionId.nullable(),
  state: DecisionState,
  sourceId: SourceId,
  withdrawnAt: Timestamp.nullable(),
  withdrawnReason: z.string().nullable(),
  createdAt: Timestamp,
  updatedAt: Timestamp,
});
export type DecisionDto = z.infer<typeof DecisionDto>;

export const PrincipleStatement = z.string().trim().min(1).max(500);

const Quote = z.string().max(2000);

/** What an agent, or 10db's answer detector, proposes; the builder picks the final scope. */
export const PrincipleDraft = z
  .object({
    statement: PrincipleStatement.describe('One rule, phrased as an instruction.'),
    kind: z
      .enum(PrincipleKind)
      .default(PrincipleKind.Heuristic)
      .describe('policy = must, heuristic = prefer, belief = context.'),
    topicKey: TopicKey.optional().describe(
      'A short stable key such as testing.no-sleeps; a later principle with the same key and scope replaces this one.',
    ),
    scope: z.enum(KnowledgeScope).default(KnowledgeScope.Project),
    area: AreaName.optional(),
    quote: Quote.optional().describe('The words this principle comes from, quoted exactly.'),
  })
  .refine((d) => (d.scope === KnowledgeScope.Area) === (d.area !== undefined), {
    message: 'area is required for scope "area" and only allowed there',
    path: ['area'],
  });
export type PrincipleDraft = z.output<typeof PrincipleDraft>;

/** A principle the builder writes directly; it is confirmed at once. */
export const PrincipleInput = z.object({
  target: KnowledgeTarget,
  statement: PrincipleStatement,
  kind: z.enum(PrincipleKind),
  topicKey: TopicKey,
  quote: Quote.optional(),
});
export type PrincipleInput = z.infer<typeof PrincipleInput>;

export const PrincipleDto = z.object({
  id: PrincipleId,
  projectId: ProjectId.nullable(),
  scope: z.enum(KnowledgeScope),
  area: z.string().nullable(),
  statement: z.string(),
  kind: z.enum(PrincipleKind),
  topicKey: z.string(),
  status: z.enum(PrincipleStatus),
  state: z.enum(KnowledgeState),
  supersedesId: PrincipleId.nullable(),
  supersededById: PrincipleId.nullable(),
  sourceId: SourceId,
  sourceQuote: z.string().nullable(),
  speaker: z.string().nullable(),
  confirmedBy: z.string().nullable(),
  confirmedAt: Timestamp.nullable(),
  retiredAt: Timestamp.nullable(),
  retiredReason: z.string().nullable(),
  withdrawnAt: Timestamp.nullable(),
  withdrawnReason: z.string().nullable(),
  createdAt: Timestamp,
  updatedAt: Timestamp,
});
export type PrincipleDto = z.infer<typeof PrincipleDto>;

/** Retiring or withdrawing a record needs a reason (spec §9: a withdrawn tombstone, never a silent delete). */
export const ReasonInput = z.object({ reason: z.string().trim().min(1).max(2000) });
export type ReasonInput = z.infer<typeof ReasonInput>;

export const ProposalDto = z.object({
  id: ProposalId,
  projectId: ProjectId,
  kind: z.enum(ProposalKind),
  title: z.string(),
  body: z.string(),
  status: z.enum(ProposalStatus),
  rationale: z.string().nullable(),
  /** The proposed principle, for a principle proposal. */
  principle: PrincipleDto.nullable(),
  sourceId: SourceId,
  createdAt: Timestamp,
  updatedAt: Timestamp,
});
export type ProposalDto = z.infer<typeof ProposalDto>;

/** Accepting a proposal; for a principle, any field given replaces the draft's before it is confirmed. */
export const ProposalAccept = z.object({
  target: KnowledgeTarget.optional(),
  statement: PrincipleStatement.optional(),
  kind: z.enum(PrincipleKind).optional(),
  topicKey: TopicKey.optional(),
  note: z.string().max(4000).optional(),
});
export type ProposalAccept = z.infer<typeof ProposalAccept>;

export const ProposalReject = z.object({ note: z.string().max(4000).optional() });
export type ProposalReject = z.infer<typeof ProposalReject>;

/** Where a record came from, resolved from its `sources` row (spec §9 provenance). */
export const ProvenanceDto = z.object({
  sourceKind: z.enum(SourceKind),
  recordedAt: Timestamp,
  by: z.string(),
  inbox: z
    .object({
      id: InboxItemId,
      title: z.string(),
      choice: z.string().nullable(),
      note: z.string().nullable(),
    })
    .nullable(),
  session: z
    .object({ id: SessionId, workstreamId: WorkstreamId, taskTitle: z.string().nullable() })
    .nullable(),
  quote: z.string().nullable(),
  speaker: z.string().nullable(),
  confirmedBy: z.string().nullable(),
  confirmedAt: Timestamp.nullable(),
});
export type ProvenanceDto = z.infer<typeof ProvenanceDto>;

const Flag = z
  .enum(['0', '1'])
  .default('0')
  .transform((v) => v === '1');

/** The list routes' query string. `history` also returns superseded, retired, withdrawn and proposed records. */
export const KnowledgeQuery = z.object({
  projectId: ProjectId,
  scope: z.enum(KnowledgeScope).optional(),
  area: AreaName.optional(),
  kind: z.enum(PrincipleKind).optional(),
  q: z.string().trim().max(200).optional(),
  history: Flag,
});
export type KnowledgeQuery = z.output<typeof KnowledgeQuery>;

export const ProposalQuery = z.object({
  projectId: ProjectId,
  status: z.enum(ProposalStatus).default(ProposalStatus.Pending),
});
export type ProposalQuery = z.output<typeof ProposalQuery>;

/** The detail key of a "review this principle?" card. */
export const PRINCIPLE_REVIEW_DETAIL = 'principleReview';
```

### Task 2: The principles table, the knowledge columns and the principles FTS index

**Files:**

- Modify: `packages/db/src/schema.ts`, `packages/db/AGENTS.md`
- Create: `packages/db/drizzle/0002_principles_fts.sql` (with `drizzle-kit generate --custom`, then written once)
- Test: `packages/db/src/schema.test.ts`
- Generated: `packages/db/drizzle/` (baseline regenerated)

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `PrincipleId`, `KnowledgeScope`, `PrincipleKind`, `PrincipleStatus` (Task 1).
- Produces:
  - `schema.principles` with columns `id`, `projectId` (nullable), `scope`, `area`, `statement`, `kind`, `topicKey`, `status`, `supersedesId`, `sourceId`, `sourceQuote`, `speaker`, `confirmedBy`, `confirmedAt`, `retiredAt`, `retiredBy`, `retiredReason`, `withdrawnAt`, `withdrawnBy`, `withdrawnReason`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, index `principles_topic_idx` on (`topic_key`, `scope`, `project_id`, `area`) and index `principles_supersedes_idx` on `supersedes_id`.
  - `schema.decisions` gains `withdrawnAt`, `withdrawnBy`, `withdrawnReason` and index `decisions_supersedes_idx` on `supersedes_id`; its `scope` is typed `KnowledgeScope`. The successor lookup in Tasks 3 and 4 runs a subquery on `supersedes_id` for every row it reads, so without these indexes a list is quadratic.
  - `schema.proposals.principleId` and `schema.deviations.principleId` (nullable, referencing `principles`).
  - The FTS5 table `principles_fts(statement, topic_key, source_quote)` kept in step by triggers.

function indexNames(table: string): unknown[] {

```ts
/** Spec §4 `principles`. A row's state (current, superseded, …) is derived on read, never stored. */
export const principles = sqliteTable(
  'principles',
  {
    id: text('id').$type<PrincipleId>().primaryKey(),
    /** Null for a `you` principle, which applies in every project. */
    projectId: text('project_id')
      .$type<ProjectId>()
      .references(() => projects.id),
    scope: text('scope').$type<KnowledgeScope>().notNull(),
    area: text('area'),
    statement: text('statement').notNull(),
    kind: text('kind').$type<PrincipleKind>().notNull(),
    topicKey: text('topic_key').notNull(),
    status: text('status').$type<PrincipleStatus>().notNull(),
    supersedesId: text('supersedes_id').$type<PrincipleId>(),
    sourceId: text('source_id')
      .$type<SourceId>()
      .notNull()
      .references(() => sources.id),
    sourceQuote: text('source_quote'),
    speaker: text('speaker'),
    confirmedBy: text('confirmed_by'),
    confirmedAt: text('confirmed_at'),
    retiredAt: text('retired_at'),
    retiredBy: text('retired_by'),
    retiredReason: text('retired_reason'),
    withdrawnAt: text('withdrawn_at'),
    withdrawnBy: text('withdrawn_by'),
    withdrawnReason: text('withdrawn_reason'),
    createdAt: createdAt(),
    updatedAt: updatedAt(),
  },
  (t) => [
    index('principles_topic_idx').on(t.topicKey, t.scope, t.projectId, t.area),
    // Every read derives state by looking up a successor through supersedes_id.
    index('principles_supersedes_idx').on(t.supersedesId),
  ],
);
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Regenerate the migration baseline and write the principles FTS migration**

Until `v0.1.0` the baseline is regenerated, not extended (`packages/db/AGENTS.md`). Run in `packages/db`:

```bash
cp drizzle/0001_decisions_fts.sql "$TMPDIR/decisions_fts.sql" && rm -rf drizzle && pnpm exec drizzle-kit generate --name init && pnpm exec drizzle-kit generate --custom --name decisions_fts && cp "$TMPDIR/decisions_fts.sql" drizzle/0001_decisions_fts.sql && pnpm exec drizzle-kit generate --custom --name principles_fts
```

Expected: `drizzle/0000_init.sql` creates `principles` with `principles_topic_idx` and `principles_supersedes_idx`, `decisions_supersedes_idx`, and the new columns on `decisions`, `proposals` and `deviations` (and still everything M2b Part A and batch delivery added); `git diff packages/db/drizzle/0001_decisions_fts.sql` prints nothing; an empty `drizzle/0002_principles_fts.sql` exists. Fill it in, exactly:

```sql
-- Keyed on the implicit rowid of `principles`, like decisions_fts: VACUUM may renumber implicit rowids,
-- so never VACUUM this database without rebuilding both indexes afterwards. Backups use .backup(), which is safe.
CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE principles_fts USING fts5(statement, topic_key, source_quote, content='principles', content_rowid='rowid');
--> statement-breakpoint
CREATE TRIGGER principles_fts_insert AFTER INSERT ON principles BEGIN
  INSERT INTO principles_fts(rowid, statement, topic_key, source_quote) VALUES (new.rowid, new.statement, new.topic_key, new.source_quote);
END;
--> statement-breakpoint
CREATE TRIGGER principles_fts_delete AFTER DELETE ON principles BEGIN
  INSERT INTO principles_fts(principles_fts, rowid, statement, topic_key, source_quote) VALUES ('delete', old.rowid, old.statement, old.topic_key, old.source_quote);
END;
--> statement-breakpoint
CREATE TRIGGER principles_fts_update AFTER UPDATE ON principles BEGIN
  INSERT INTO principles_fts(principles_fts, rowid, statement, topic_key, source_quote) VALUES ('delete', old.rowid, old.statement, old.topic_key, old.source_quote);
  INSERT INTO principles_fts(rowid, statement, topic_key, source_quote) VALUES (new.rowid, new.statement, new.topic_key, new.source_quote);
END;
```

From now on this file is authored, like `0001_decisions_fts.sql`: a later baseline regeneration saves and restores it byte for byte.

Update the baseline paragraph of `packages/db/AGENTS.md` to:

```markdown
- **Until the first published release (`v0.1.0`), the baseline is regenerated, not extended.** The
  baseline is `0000_init` (generated) plus two hand-written FTS5 migrations made with
  `drizzle-kit generate --custom` and kept byte for byte: `0001_decisions_fts` and
  `0002_principles_fts`. To change the schema: save both SQL files, delete `drizzle/`, run
  `pnpm exec drizzle-kit generate --name init`, then `pnpm exec drizzle-kit generate --custom --name
decisions_fts` and `pnpm exec drizzle-kit generate --custom --name principles_fts` in this package,
  and put the saved SQL back. Nothing migrates an old database: `migrateDatabase` refuses one made
  under an older baseline (`StaleSchemaBaselineError`), the daemon exits with code 3, and the builder
  deletes the data directory by hand.
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`packages/db/src/store/decisions.test.ts`:

```ts
import type { DecisionId, ProjectId } from '@10db/contracts';
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import {
  getDecision,
  insertDecision,
  listDecisions,
  supersedeDecision,
  withdrawDecision,
  type NewDecision,
} from './decisions';
import { insertSource } from './knowledge';
import {
  checkKnowledgeIndex,
  rebuildKnowledgeIndex,
  searchDecisionIds,
  searchDecisions,
} from './knowledge-search';
import type { Store } from './store';
import { createTestStore, seedProject } from './test-support';

let store: Store;
let close: () => void;
beforeEach(() => ({ store, close } = createTestStore()));
afterEach(() => {
  close();
});

const decision = (projectId: ProjectId | null, overrides: Partial<NewDecision> = {}) =>
  store.write((ctx) => ({
    projectId,
    question: 'Which queue library?',
    choice: 'BullMQ',
    rationale: 'Redis is already deployed',
    decidedBy: 'me',
    scope: projectId === null ? ('you' as const) : ('project' as const),
    area: null,
    sourceId: insertSource(ctx, 'builder', {}),
    ...overrides,
  })).value;

const record = (projectId: ProjectId | null, overrides: Partial<NewDecision> = {}): DecisionId =>
  store.write((ctx) => insertDecision(ctx, decision(projectId, overrides))).value;

const found = (projectId: ProjectId, query: string) =>
  searchDecisions(store.db, projectId, query, 10).map((d) => d.choice);

/** A ← B ← C: BullMQ, superseded by pg-boss, superseded by SQS. */
const chain = (projectId: ProjectId) => {
  const a = record(projectId);
  const b = store.write((ctx) =>
    supersedeDecision(ctx, a, decision(projectId, { choice: 'pg-boss' })),
  ).value;
  const c = store.write((ctx) =>
    supersedeDecision(ctx, b, decision(projectId, { choice: 'SQS' })),
  ).value;
  return { a, b, c };
};

describe('decisions', () => {
  it('reads a recorded decision back through search, current and newest first', () => {
    const project = seedProject(store);
    const first = record(project.id);
    const second = record(project.id, { question: 'Which queue for jobs?', choice: 'pg-boss' });
    expect(found(project.id, 'queue')).toEqual(expect.arrayContaining(['BullMQ', 'pg-boss']));
    expect(listDecisions(store.db, { projectId: project.id }).map((d) => d.id)).toEqual([
      second,
      first,
    ]);
    expect(getDecision(store.db, first)).toMatchObject({
      state: 'current',
      supersededById: null,
      withdrawnAt: null,
    });
  });

  it('applies a decision of yours in every project, and a project decision in its own only', () => {
    const a = seedProject(store);
    const b = seedProject(store);
    record(null, { choice: 'Conventional Commits', question: 'Which commit style?' });
    record(a.id, { choice: 'Squash merges', question: 'Which merge style?' });
    expect(found(b.id, 'commit')).toEqual(['Conventional Commits']);
    expect(found(b.id, 'merge')).toEqual([]);
    expect(found(a.id, 'merge')).toEqual(['Squash merges']);
  });

  it('supersedes a current decision: search returns only the successor, history both', () => {
    const project = seedProject(store);
    const old = record(project.id);
    const next = store.write((ctx) =>
      supersedeDecision(
        ctx,
        old,
        decision(project.id, { choice: 'pg-boss', rationale: 'one database' }),
      ),
    ).value;
    expect(found(project.id, 'queue')).toEqual(['pg-boss']);
    expect(getDecision(store.db, old)).toMatchObject({ state: 'superseded', supersededById: next });
    expect(getDecision(store.db, next)).toMatchObject({ state: 'current', supersedesId: old });
    expect(
      searchDecisionIds(store.db, {
        projectId: project.id,
        query: 'queue',
        limit: 10,
        history: true,
      }),
    ).toEqual(expect.arrayContaining([old, next]));
  });

  it.each([
    { name: 'a superseded decision', setup: 'supersede' },
    { name: 'a withdrawn decision', setup: 'withdraw' },
  ] as const)('refuses to supersede $name', ({ setup }) => {
    const project = seedProject(store);
    const old = record(project.id);
    store.write((ctx) =>
      setup === 'supersede'
        ? supersedeDecision(ctx, old, decision(project.id, { choice: 'pg-boss' }))
        : withdrawDecision(ctx, old, 'me', 'wrong'),
    );
    expect(() =>
      store.write((ctx) => supersedeDecision(ctx, old, decision(project.id, { choice: 'x' }))),
    ).toThrow(/only a current decision/);
  });

  it('withdraws a decision as a tombstone that search skips', () => {
    const project = seedProject(store);
    const id = record(project.id);
    const withdrawn = store.write((ctx) =>
      withdrawDecision(ctx, id, 'me', 'recorded by mistake'),
    ).value;
    expect(withdrawn).toMatchObject({ state: 'withdrawn', withdrawnReason: 'recorded by mistake' });
    expect(found(project.id, 'queue')).toEqual([]);
    expect(listDecisions(store.db, { projectId: project.id })).toEqual([]);
    expect(
      listDecisions(store.db, { projectId: project.id, history: true }).map((d) => d.id),
    ).toEqual([id]);
  });

  it.each([
    {
      name: 'withdrawing a superseded record is refused (the middle of a three-link chain)',
      before: [],
      refused: 'b',
      current: 'SQS',
    },
    {
      name: 'withdrawing a superseded record is refused (the root of a three-link chain)',
      before: [],
      refused: 'a',
      current: 'SQS',
    },
    {
      name: 'withdrawing a withdrawn record is refused',
      before: ['c'],
      refused: 'c',
      current: 'pg-boss',
    },
  ] as const)('$name, and one link stays current', ({ before, refused, current }) => {
    const project = seedProject(store);
    const links = chain(project.id);
    for (const link of before)
      store.write((ctx) => withdrawDecision(ctx, links[link], 'me', 'reverted'));
    expect(() =>
      store.write((ctx) => withdrawDecision(ctx, links[refused], 'me', 'no longer true')),
    ).toThrow(/only a current decision can be withdrawn/);
    expect(found(project.id, 'queue')).toEqual([current]);
  });

  it('makes only the link before the head current when the head of a three-link chain is withdrawn', () => {
    const project = seedProject(store);
    const { a, b, c } = chain(project.id);
    store.write((ctx) => withdrawDecision(ctx, c, 'me', 'reverted'));
    expect(found(project.id, 'queue')).toEqual(['pg-boss']);
    expect([a, b, c].map((id) => getDecision(store.db, id).state)).toEqual([
      'superseded',
      'current',
      'withdrawn',
    ]);
  });

  it.each([
    { name: 'scope you', filter: { scope: 'you' as const }, expected: ['Conventional Commits'] },
    { name: 'scope project', filter: { scope: 'project' as const }, expected: ['Squash merges'] },
    { name: 'area api', filter: { scope: 'area' as const, area: 'api' }, expected: ['Hono'] },
    { name: 'area ui', filter: { scope: 'area' as const, area: 'ui' }, expected: [] },
  ])('filters the list by $name', ({ filter, expected }) => {
    const project = seedProject(store);
    record(null, { choice: 'Conventional Commits' });
    record(project.id, { choice: 'Squash merges' });
    record(project.id, { scope: 'area', area: 'api', choice: 'Hono' });
    expect(
      listDecisions(store.db, { projectId: project.id, ...filter }).map((d) => d.choice),
    ).toEqual(expected);
  });

  it('lists the decisions named by ids in that order', () => {
    const project = seedProject(store);
    const a = record(project.id, { choice: 'A' });
    const b = record(project.id, { choice: 'B' });
    expect(
      listDecisions(store.db, { projectId: project.id, ids: [a, b] }).map((d) => d.choice),
    ).toEqual(['A', 'B']);
    expect(listDecisions(store.db, { projectId: project.id, ids: [] })).toEqual([]);
  });

  it('detects an index out of step with its table and rebuilds it', () => {
    const project = seedProject(store);
    expect(checkKnowledgeIndex(store.db)).toEqual([]);
    store.db.$client.exec('DROP TRIGGER decisions_fts_insert');
    record(project.id);
    expect(found(project.id, 'queue')).toEqual([]);
    expect(checkKnowledgeIndex(store.db)).toEqual(['decisions_fts']);
    rebuildKnowledgeIndex(store.db, 'decisions_fts');
    expect(checkKnowledgeIndex(store.db)).toEqual([]);
    expect(found(project.id, 'queue')).toEqual(['BullMQ']);
  });
});
```

```ts
import {
  ConflictError,
  DecisionDto,
  EventType,
  KnowledgeState,
  mintId,
  NotFoundError,
  type DecisionId,
  type KnowledgeScope,
  type ProjectId,
  type SourceId,
} from '@10db/contracts';
import { eq, sql, type SQL } from 'drizzle-orm';
import * as schema from '../schema';
import type { DbReader, WriteContext } from './store';

export interface NewDecision {
  readonly projectId: ProjectId | null;
  readonly question: string;
  readonly choice: string;
  readonly rationale: string;
  readonly decidedBy: string;
  readonly scope: KnowledgeScope;
  readonly area: string | null;
  readonly sourceId: SourceId;
}

/** The successor that supersedes `d`: the newest decision naming it that is not withdrawn. */
const SUCCESSOR = sql`(SELECT s.id FROM decisions s WHERE s.supersedes_id = d.id AND s.withdrawn_at IS NULL ORDER BY s.id DESC LIMIT 1)`;

/** True for a current decision (spec §4: current unless withdrawn or superseded). */
export const currentDecisionSql = sql`(d.withdrawn_at IS NULL AND ${SUCCESSOR} IS NULL)`;

// Column aliases match DecisionDto, so each row parses as one; the state is derived here, never stored.
const SELECT_DECISION = sql`
  SELECT d.id AS id, d.project_id AS projectId, d.scope AS scope, d.area AS area,
    d.question AS question, d.choice AS choice, d.rationale AS rationale, d.decided_by AS decidedBy,
    d.supersedes_id AS supersedesId, ${SUCCESSOR} AS supersededById,
    CASE WHEN d.withdrawn_at IS NOT NULL THEN ${KnowledgeState.Withdrawn}
         WHEN ${SUCCESSOR} IS NOT NULL THEN ${KnowledgeState.Superseded}
         ELSE ${KnowledgeState.Current} END AS state,
    d.source_id AS sourceId, d.withdrawn_at AS withdrawnAt, d.withdrawn_reason AS withdrawnReason,
    d.created_at AS createdAt, d.updated_at AS updatedAt
  FROM decisions d`;

export function insertDecision(
  ctx: WriteContext,
  input: NewDecision,
  supersedesId: DecisionId | null = null,
): DecisionId {
  const id = mintId('Decision');
  ctx.tx
    .insert(schema.decisions)
    .values({ id, ...input, supersedesId, createdAt: ctx.now, updatedAt: ctx.now })
    .run();
  ctx.emit({
    type: EventType.DecisionRecorded,
    entityType: 'decision',
    entityId: id,
    data: { projectId: input.projectId, question: input.question, supersedesId },
  });
  return id;
}

export function getDecision(db: DbReader, id: DecisionId): DecisionDto {
  const row = DecisionDto.array().parse(db.all(sql`${SELECT_DECISION} WHERE d.id = ${id}`))[0];
  if (!row) throw new NotFoundError('decision', id);
  return row;
}

export interface DecisionFilter {
  readonly projectId: ProjectId;
  readonly scope?: KnowledgeScope;
  readonly area?: string;
  /** Also superseded and withdrawn decisions. */
  readonly history?: boolean;
  /** Only these, in this order. */
  readonly ids?: readonly DecisionId[];
}

/** The project's decisions and the builder's own, newest first (or in `ids` order); current ones unless `history`. */
export function listDecisions(db: DbReader, filter: DecisionFilter): DecisionDto[] {
  if (filter.ids?.length === 0) return [];
  const where: SQL[] = [sql`(d.project_id = ${filter.projectId} OR d.project_id IS NULL)`];
  if (filter.scope) where.push(sql`d.scope = ${filter.scope}`);
  if (filter.area) where.push(sql`d.area = ${filter.area}`);
  if (!filter.history) where.push(currentDecisionSql);
  if (filter.ids)
    where.push(
      sql`d.id IN (${sql.join(
        filter.ids.map((id) => sql`${id}`),
        sql`, `,
      )})`,
    );
  const rows = DecisionDto.array().parse(
    db.all(sql`${SELECT_DECISION} WHERE ${sql.join(where, sql` AND `)} ORDER BY d.id DESC`),
  );
  if (!filter.ids) return rows;
  const order = new Map(filter.ids.map((id, i) => [id, i]));
  return rows.toSorted((a, b) => (order.get(a.id) ?? 0) - (order.get(b.id) ?? 0));
}

/** Records `input` as the successor of a current decision (the builder's explicit supersede). */
export function supersedeDecision(
  ctx: WriteContext,
  id: DecisionId,
  input: NewDecision,
): DecisionId {
  const old = getDecision(ctx.tx, id);
  if (old.state !== KnowledgeState.Current)
    throw new ConflictError(
      `Decision ${id} is ${old.state}; only a current decision can be superseded`,
    );
  const next = insertDecision(ctx, input, id);
  ctx.emit({
    type: EventType.DecisionChanged,
    entityType: 'decision',
    entityId: id,
    data: { supersededBy: next },
  });
  return next;
}

/**
 * Withdraws the current decision: the row stays as a tombstone with who and why (spec §9). Only the
 * head of a chain can go; withdrawing a superseded link would make its predecessor current beside its
 * successor.
 */
export function withdrawDecision(
  ctx: WriteContext,
  id: DecisionId,
  by: string,
  reason: string,
): DecisionDto {
  const current = getDecision(ctx.tx, id);
  if (current.state !== KnowledgeState.Current)
    throw new ConflictError(
      `Decision ${id} is ${current.state}; only a current decision can be withdrawn`,
    );
  ctx.tx
    .update(schema.decisions)
    .set({ withdrawnAt: ctx.now, withdrawnBy: by, withdrawnReason: reason, updatedAt: ctx.now })
    .where(eq(schema.decisions.id, id))
    .run();
  ctx.emit({
    type: EventType.DecisionChanged,
    entityType: 'decision',
    entityId: id,
    data: { withdrawn: true, by },
  });
  return getDecision(ctx.tx, id);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the search module**

`packages/db/src/store/knowledge-search.ts`:

```ts
import { DecisionId, type DecisionDto, type KnowledgeScope, type ProjectId } from '@10db/contracts';
import { sql, type SQL } from 'drizzle-orm';
import { z } from 'zod';
import type { TendbDatabase } from '../database';
import { currentDecisionSql, listDecisions } from './decisions';
import type { DbReader } from './store';

// Every full-text query lives here, so the storage engine can change later (packages/db/AGENTS.md).

/** Turns free text into an FTS5 query of quoted terms joined by OR, so user text is never parsed as syntax. */
function ftsQuery(text: string): string | null {
  const terms = text.toLowerCase().match(/[\p{L}\p{N}]+/gu) ?? [];
  return terms.length === 0 ? null : terms.map((t) => `"${t}"`).join(' OR ');
}

export interface SearchQuery {
  readonly projectId: ProjectId;
  readonly query: string;
  readonly limit: number;
  readonly scope?: KnowledgeScope;
  readonly area?: string;
  /** Also superseded and withdrawn records. */
  readonly history?: boolean;
}

const DecisionIdRows = z.array(z.object({ id: DecisionId }));

function narrow(alias: string, q: SearchQuery): SQL[] {
  const column = (name: string) => sql.raw(`${alias}.${name}`);
  const parts: SQL[] = [
    sql`(${column('project_id')} = ${q.projectId} OR ${column('project_id')} IS NULL)`,
  ];
  if (q.scope) parts.push(sql`${column('scope')} = ${q.scope}`);
  if (q.area) parts.push(sql`${column('area')} = ${q.area}`);
  return parts;
}

/** Decisions matching the query, best first; current ones unless `history`. */
export function searchDecisionIds(db: DbReader, q: SearchQuery): DecisionId[] {
  const match = ftsQuery(q.query);
  if (match === null) return [];
  const where = [sql`decisions_fts MATCH ${match}`, ...narrow('d', q)];
  if (!q.history) where.push(currentDecisionSql);
  const rows = db.all(sql`
    SELECT d.id AS id FROM decisions_fts f JOIN decisions d ON d.rowid = f.rowid
    WHERE ${sql.join(where, sql` AND `)}
    ORDER BY bm25(decisions_fts), d.id
    LIMIT ${q.limit}`);
  return DecisionIdRows.parse(rows).map((r) => r.id);
}

/** The current decisions in scope for the project that match `query`, best first. */
export function searchDecisions(
  db: DbReader,
  projectId: ProjectId,
  query: string,
  limit: number,
): DecisionDto[] {
  const ids = searchDecisionIds(db, { projectId, query, limit });
  return listDecisions(db, { projectId, ids });
}

export const KnowledgeIndex = { Decisions: 'decisions_fts', Principles: 'principles_fts' } as const;
export type KnowledgeIndex = (typeof KnowledgeIndex)[keyof typeof KnowledgeIndex];

function sqliteCode(error: unknown): string | null {
  return error instanceof Error && 'code' in error && typeof error.code === 'string'
    ? error.code
    : null;
}

/**
 * The FTS indexes that disagree with their tables. FTS5's integrity-check with rank 1 also compares
 * the index with its content table; a mismatch is SQLITE_CORRUPT_VTAB.
 */
export function checkKnowledgeIndex(db: TendbDatabase): KnowledgeIndex[] {
  const broken: KnowledgeIndex[] = [];
  for (const index of Object.values(KnowledgeIndex)) {
    try {
      db.$client
        .prepare(`INSERT INTO ${index}(${index}, rank) VALUES ('integrity-check', 1)`)
        .run();
    } catch (error) {
      if (!sqliteCode(error)?.startsWith('SQLITE_CORRUPT')) throw error;
      broken.push(index);
    }
  }
  return broken;
}

/** Rebuilds an FTS index from its content table. */
export function rebuildKnowledgeIndex(db: TendbDatabase, index: KnowledgeIndex): void {
  db.$client.prepare(`INSERT INTO ${index}(${index}) VALUES ('rebuild')`).run();
}
```

The table names come from the `KnowledgeIndex` constant, never from input, so interpolating them is safe. Add `export * from './decisions';` and `export * from './knowledge-search';` to `packages/db/src/store/index.ts`.
